  "short_description": {
    "id": "Chaac adalah dewa hujan, guntur, dan petir bangsa Maya yang menghantam awan dengan kapak petirnya.",
    "en": "Chaac is the Maya god of rain, thunder, and lightning, who strikes the clouds with his lightning axe.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "chaac-c01",
      "chaac-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Chaac, juga ditulis Chac atau Chaahk dalam bahasa Maya Klasik, adalah dewa hujan, guntur, dan petir bangsa Maya. Dengan kapak petirnya ia menghantam awan sehingga menghasilkan guntur dan hujan. Ia biasanya digambarkan bertubuh manusia bersisik reptil atau amfibi, berkepala bertaring dengan hidung panjang menggantung, dan sering membawa perisai serta kapak petir.",
      "en": "Chaac, also written Chac or, in Classic Mayan, Chaahk, is the Maya deity of rain, thunder, and lightning. Wielding a lightning axe, he hits the clouds so that thunder and rain come from them. He is usually shown with a human body covered in reptilian or amphibian scales, a fanged head with a long drooping nose, and he often carries a shield and a lightning axe.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chaac-c01",
        "chaac-c05",
        "chaac-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Empat Chaac berdiam di empat arah mata angin dan mengenakan warna penjurunya. Sebuah sumber menyebutnya sebagian manusia dan sebagian reptil yang menurut kepercayaan tinggal di gua, ditakuti sekaligus disembah. Ia membawa hujan yang dibutuhkan, tetapi juga banjir dan petir yang mengancam, dan menuntut korban darah sebagai bayaran atas hujan.",
      "en": "Four Chaacs live in the four cardinal directions and wear their directional colors. One source calls him part human and part reptile, said to live in caves, both feared and worshipped. He brings needed rain but also floods and threatening lightning, and demands blood sacrifices as payment for rain.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chaac-c03",
        "chaac-c13",
        "chaac-c14",
        "chaac-c15"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Chaac disebut pelindung pertanian, dan mitos terkenal tentang para Chaac menyangkut dibukanya gunung tempat jagung disembunyikan. Sumber masa Klasik menunjukkan Chaac sendiri yang membukanya dengan kapak petir. Ia dipadankan dengan Tlaloc pada bangsa Aztec dan Cocijo pada bangsa Zapotec. Hingga kini ia tetap penting bagi sebagian kelompok Maya di Meksiko.",
      "en": "The rain deity is called a patron of agriculture, and a well-known myth of the Chaacs concerns the opening of the mountain where maize was hidden. Classic-period sources suggest Chaac himself opened it with his lightning axe. He corresponds to Tlaloc among the Aztecs and Cocijo among the Zapotecs. He still matters to some Maya groups in Mexico today.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chaac-c02",
        "chaac-c07",
        "chaac-c08",
        "chaac-c12"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Petani Maya Yucatec masa kini membedakan banyak segi hujan dan awan serta mempersonifikasikannya sebagai dewa-dewa hujan berjenjang, dan upacara Chʼa Cháak memohon hujan dengan jamuan seremonial bagi mereka. Chaac juga tetap penting bagi sebagian kelompok Maya di Meksiko.",
    "en": "Contemporary Yucatec Maya farmers distinguish many aspects of rain and clouds and personify them as ranked rain deities, and the Chʼa Cháak ceremony asks for rain with a ceremonial banquet for them. Chaac also remains important to some Maya groups in Mexico.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "chaac-c04",
      "chaac-c09",
      "chaac-c12"
    ]
  },

    {
      "id": "chaac-c04",
      "source_id": "chaac-s1",
      "quote": "Contemporary Yucatec Maya farmers distinguish many more aspects of the rainfall and the clouds and personify them as different, hierarchically-ordered rain deities.",
      "locator": "Rain deities and rain makers",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Petani Maya Yucatec masa kini membedakan lebih banyak segi hujan dan awan serta mempersonifikasikannya sebagai dewa-dewa hujan yang berjenjang.",
        "en": "Contemporary Yucatec Maya farmers distinguish many more aspects of the rainfall and the clouds and personify them as different, hierarchically ordered rain deities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c05",
      "source_id": "chaac-s1",
      "quote": "Chaac (also spelled Chac or, in Classic Mayan, Chaahk ... is the name of the Maya god of rain, thunder, and lightning.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chaac (juga ditulis Chac atau, dalam bahasa Maya Klasik, Chaahk) adalah nama dewa hujan, guntur, dan petir bangsa Maya.",
        "en": "Chaac (also spelled Chac or, in Classic Mayan, Chaahk) is the name of the Maya god of rain, thunder, and lightning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c06",
      "source_id": "chaac-s1",
      "quote": "Chaac is usually depicted with a human body showing reptilian or amphibian scales, and with a non-human head evincing fangs and a long, pendulous nose. In the Classic style, a shell serves as his ear ornament. He often carries a shield and a lightning axe, the axe being personified by a closely related deity, K'awiil, called Bolon Dzacab in Yucatec.",
      "locator": "Iconography",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chaac biasanya digambarkan bertubuh manusia dengan sisik reptil atau amfibi, berkepala bukan manusia dengan taring dan hidung panjang menggantung. Dalam gaya Klasik, cangkang menjadi hiasan telinganya. Ia sering membawa perisai dan kapak petir; kapak itu dipersonifikasikan oleh dewa yang berkerabat dekat, K'awiil, yang disebut Bolon Dzacab dalam bahasa Yucatec.",
        "en": "Chaac is usually depicted with a human body showing reptilian or amphibian scales and a non-human head with fangs and a long, pendulous nose; in the Classic style a shell is his ear ornament, and he often carries a shield and a lightning axe, the axe being personified by the closely related deity K'awiil, called Bolon Dzacab in Yucatec."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c07",
      "source_id": "chaac-s1",
      "quote": "The rain deity is a patron of agriculture. A well-known myth involving the Chaacs (or related rain and lightning deities) is about the opening of the mountain where the maize was hidden.",
      "locator": "Mythology",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewa hujan adalah pelindung pertanian. Mitos terkenal yang melibatkan para Chaac (atau dewa hujan dan petir terkait) adalah tentang terbukanya gunung tempat jagung disembunyikan.",
        "en": "The rain deity is a patron of agriculture, and a well-known myth involving the Chaacs (or related rain and lightning deities) concerns the opening of the mountain where the maize was hidden."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c08",
      "source_id": "chaac-s1",
      "quote": "Classic period Maya sources also suggest that Chaac was the god who opened the mountain containing maize, using his lightning axe, K'awiil.",
      "locator": "Mythology",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber Maya masa Klasik juga menunjukkan bahwa Chaac adalah dewa yang membuka gunung berisi jagung dengan kapak petirnya, K'awiil.",
        "en": "Classic period Maya sources also suggest that Chaac was the god who opened the mountain containing maize, using his lightning axe, K'awiil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c09",
      "source_id": "chaac-s1",
      "quote": "Among the rituals for the rain deities, the Yucatec Chʼa Cháak ceremony for asking rain centers on a ceremonial banquet for the rain deities. It includes four boys (one for each cardinal point) acting and chanting as frogs.",
      "locator": "Rain rituals",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di antara ritus bagi dewa-dewa hujan, upacara Chʼa Cháak orang Yucatec untuk memohon hujan berpusat pada jamuan seremonial bagi dewa hujan, dengan empat anak laki-laki (satu untuk tiap arah mata angin) yang berperan dan melantunkan seruan seperti katak.",
        "en": "Among the rituals for the rain deities, the Yucatec Chʼa Cháak ceremony for asking rain centers on a ceremonial banquet for the rain deities and includes four boys, one for each cardinal point, acting and chanting as frogs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c10",
      "source_id": "chaac-s1",
      "quote": "Later, Chaac commits adultery with his brother's wife and is duly punished; his tears of agony give origin to the rain.",
      "locator": "Mythology",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kemudian Chaac berzina dengan istri saudaranya dan dihukum sepantasnya; air mata kesakitannya menjadi asal hujan.",
        "en": "Later, Chaac commits adultery with his brother's wife and is duly punished; his tears of agony give origin to the rain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c11",
      "source_id": "chaac-s1",
      "quote": "A large part of one of the four surviving Maya codices, the Dresden Codex, is dedicated to the Chaacs, their locations, and activities. It illustrates the intimate relationship existing between the Chaacs, the Bacabs, and the aged goddess, Ixchel.",
      "locator": "Rain",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian besar salah satu dari empat kodeks Maya yang tersisa, Kodeks Dresden, dikhususkan bagi para Chaac, lokasi, dan kegiatan mereka. Kodeks itu memperlihatkan hubungan erat antara para Chaac, para Bacab, dan dewi tua Ixchel.",
        "en": "A large part of the Dresden Codex, one of the four surviving Maya codices, is dedicated to the Chaacs, their locations, and activities, and illustrates the intimate relationship between the Chaacs, the Bacabs, and the aged goddess Ixchel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c12",
      "source_id": "chaac-s1",
      "quote": "Chaac continues to hold importance for some Maya groups in Mexico. In 2024, a statue of the Greek god Poseidon located in Progreso, Yucatán, caused controversy for locals who deemed it offensive to their beliefs in Chaac.",
      "locator": "In modern times",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chaac tetap penting bagi sebagian kelompok Maya di Meksiko. Pada 2024, patung dewa Yunani Poseidon di Progreso, Yucatán, menimbulkan kontroversi di kalangan warga yang menganggapnya menyinggung kepercayaan mereka kepada Chaac.",
        "en": "Chaac continues to hold importance for some Maya groups in Mexico; in 2024 a statue of the Greek god Poseidon in Progreso, Yucatán, caused controversy among locals who found it offensive to their beliefs in Chaac."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c13",
      "source_id": "chaac-s2",
      "quote": "Chak, the seeming counterpart of K'inich Ajaw, is the rain god or God B. Chak is both part human and part reptile and he is usually shown with a lightning bolt, a serpent, or an axe.",
      "locator": "Chak",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chak (ejaan sumber ini; ditulis Chaac di tempat lain), yang tampak sebagai padanan K'inich Ajaw, adalah dewa hujan atau Dewa B. Ia sebagian manusia dan sebagian reptil, dan biasanya ditampilkan dengan petir, ular, atau kapak.",
        "en": "Chak (this source's spelling), the seeming counterpart of K'inich Ajaw, is the rain god or God B; part human and part reptile, he is usually shown with a lightning bolt, a serpent, or an axe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c14",
      "source_id": "chaac-s2",
      "quote": "The Maya believe that Chak lived in caves where he would make lightning, thunder, and clouds.",
      "locator": "Chak",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Maya percaya bahwa Chak (ejaan sumber ini) tinggal di gua tempat ia membuat petir, guntur, dan awan.",
        "en": "The Maya believe that Chak (this source's spelling) lived in caves where he would make lightning, thunder, and clouds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c15",
      "source_id": "chaac-s2",
      "quote": "Chak, too, was both feared and worshipped. He brought the needed rains for the people, but also produced floods, threatening lightning, and behaved much like a wild storm. He also demanded blood sacrifices in payment for the rains that he provided.",
      "locator": "Chak",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chak (ejaan sumber ini) ditakuti sekaligus disembah. Ia membawa hujan yang dibutuhkan orang, tetapi juga menimbulkan banjir dan petir yang mengancam, dan berperilaku seperti badai liar. Ia juga menuntut korban darah sebagai bayaran atas hujan yang ia berikan.",
        "en": "Chak (this source's spelling) was both feared and worshipped; he brought needed rains but also produced floods and threatening lightning and behaved much like a wild storm, and demanded blood sacrifices in payment for the rains he provided."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c16",
      "source_id": "chaac-s3",
      "quote": "Chaac ou chác. s. foudre, tonnerre, orage, pluie;—nom générique des divinités protectrices des eaux et des moissons",
      "locator": "Vocabulary (CH)",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kosakata Maya pada sumber ini, Chaac (chác) berarti petir, guntur, badai, dan hujan, dan merupakan nama umum bagi dewa-dewa pelindung perairan dan panen.",
        "en": "In the Maya vocabulary of this source, Chaac (chác) means lightning, thunder, storm, rain, and is the generic name of the deities protecting waters and harvests."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chaac-c17",
      "source_id": "chaac-s3",
      "quote": "Chac ou Chaac, ainsi qu'on le verra plus loin, était aussi le nom générique des dieux protecteurs de la campagne et des moissons, comme les tlaloque au Mexique.",
      "locator": "Note 115",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chac atau Chaac juga merupakan nama umum bagi dewa-dewa pelindung pedesaan dan panen, seperti tlaloque di Meksiko.",
        "en": "Chac or Chaac was also the generic name of the gods protecting the countryside and harvests, like the tlaloque in Mexico."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Dalam mitologi Aztec, Xolotl adalah dewa api dan petir yang lazim digambarkan sebagai pria berkepala anjing dan menjadi psikopomp. Ia juga disebut dewa kembar, monster, kematian, kemalangan, penyakit, dan kecacatan. Dalam kodeks dan arca Aztec ia tampil sebagai anjing atau dewa berkepala anjing, dan ia dikenal sebagai dewa anjing bangsa Mexica.",
      "en": "In Aztec mythology Xolotl is a god of fire and lightning, commonly depicted as a dog-headed man and a psychopomp.       "claim_ids": [
        "xolotl-c01",
        "xolotl-c09"

    {
      "id": "Xolotl adalah saudara kembar berwujud anjing dari Quetzalcōātl, dan ia disebut personifikasi gelap planet Venus sebagai Bintang Senja. Sebagai psikopomp ia menuntun orang mati ke Mictlān, dan anjing yang mungkin menjelmakan perannya dipercaya menuntun tuannya ke Dunia Bawah dan membantu menyeberangi perairan.",
      "en": "Xolotl is the canine twin brother of Quetzalcōātl and is described as the dark personification of Venus as the Evening Star.     {

      "en": "In the myth of the gods sacrificing themselves for the newly created sun, the gods decided to give their lives.       "id": "xolotl-c01",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s1",
      "quote": "In Aztec mythology, Xolotl ... was a god of fire and lightning. He was commonly depicted as a dog-headed man and was a psychopomp. He was also god of twins, monsters, death, misfortune, sickness, and deformities.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Aztec, Xolotl adalah dewa api dan petir yang lazim digambarkan sebagai pria berkepala anjing dan menjadi psikopomp (pengantar jiwa). Ia juga dewa kembar, monster, kematian, kemalangan, penyakit, dan kecacatan.",
        "en": "In Aztec mythology, Xolotl was a god of fire and lightning, commonly depicted as a dog-headed man and a psychopomp, and also god of twins, monsters, death, misfortune, sickness, and deformities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c03",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s1",
      "quote": "Xolotl is the canine brother and twin of Quetzalcōātl, the pair being sons of the virgin Chīmalmā. He is the dark personification of Venus as the Evening Star, and was associated with heavenly fire.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xolotl adalah saudara kembar berwujud anjing dari Quetzalcōātl; keduanya putra Chīmalmā yang masih perawan. Ia adalah personifikasi gelap planet Venus sebagai Bintang Senja (Evening Star) dan dikaitkan dengan api surgawi.",
        "en": "Xolotl is the canine brother and twin of Quetzalcōātl, the pair being sons of the virgin Chīmalmā; he is the dark personification of Venus as the Evening Star and was associated with heavenly fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c05",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s1",
      "quote": "Xolotl was the sinister god of monstrosities who wears the conch-like ehēcailacacōcatl \"twisted wind jewel\" of Quetzalcōātl. His job was to protect the sun from the dangers of the underworld.",
      "locator": "Myths and functions",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xolotl disebut dewa kengerian (monstrosities) yang sinis dan mengenakan permata angin berbentuk kerang milik Quetzalcōātl. Tugasnya melindungi matahari dari bahaya dunia bawah.",
        "en": "Xolotl was the sinister god of monstrosities who wears the conch-like \"twisted wind jewel\" of Quetzalcōātl, and his job was to protect the sun from the dangers of the underworld."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c06",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s1",
      "quote": "His empty eye sockets are explained in the legend of Teotihuacan, in which the gods decided to sacrifice themselves for the newly created sun. Xolotl withdrew from this sacrifice and wept so much that his eyes fell out of their sockets.",
      "locator": "Myths and functions",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rongga matanya yang kosong dijelaskan dalam legenda Teotihuacan: ketika para dewa memutuskan mengorbankan diri demi matahari yang baru diciptakan, Xolotl mundur dari pengorbanan itu dan menangis begitu lama hingga matanya jatuh dari rongganya.",
        "en": "Xolotl's empty eye sockets are explained by the Teotihuacan legend in which the gods decided to sacrifice themselves for the newly created sun; Xolotl withdrew from the sacrifice and wept so much that his eyes fell out of their sockets."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c07",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s1",
      "quote": "Xolotl transformed himself into a young maize plant with two stalks (xolotl), a doubled maguey plant (mexolotl), and an amphibious animal (axolotl). Xolotl is thus a master transformer. In the end, Ehecatl succeeded in finding and killing Xolotl.",
      "locator": "Myths and functions",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xolotl mengubah dirinya menjadi tanaman jagung muda berbatang dua (xolotl), tanaman maguey ganda (mexolotl), dan hewan amfibi (axolotl), sehingga disebut ahli transformasi. Pada akhirnya Ehecatl berhasil menemukan dan membunuhnya.",
        "en": "Xolotl transformed himself into a young two-stalked maize plant, a doubled maguey plant, and an amphibious animal (axolotl), and is thus a master transformer; in the end Ehecatl succeeded in finding and killing him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c08",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s1",
      "quote": "As a psychopomp, Xolotl would guide the dead on their journey to Mictlān, the afterlife in myths. His two spirit animal forms are the xoloitzcuintle breed of dog and the aquatic salamander species known as the axolotl.",
      "locator": "Myths and functions",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai psikopomp, Xolotl menuntun orang mati dalam perjalanan ke Mictlān, alam baka dalam mitos. Dua wujud hewan rohnya adalah anjing ras xoloitzcuintle dan salamander air yang disebut axolotl.",
        "en": "As a psychopomp, Xolotl would guide the dead on their journey to Mictlān, the afterlife in myths; his two spirit animal forms are the xoloitzcuintle dog breed and the aquatic salamander known as the axolotl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c09",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s2",
      "quote": "Xolotl was the dog god of the Mexica people, commonly known as the Aztecs. He is represented in codices, statuary, and other extant examples of Aztec art as a dog or a god with the head of a dog.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xolotl adalah dewa anjing bangsa Mexica, yang lazim dikenal sebagai Aztec. Dalam kodeks, arca, dan karya seni Aztec lain yang masih ada, ia digambarkan sebagai anjing atau dewa berkepala anjing.",
        "en": "Xolotl was the dog god of the Mexica people, commonly known as the Aztecs, and is represented in codices, statuary, and other surviving Aztec art as a dog or a god with a dog's head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c10",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s2",
      "quote": "While twins were generally seen as a bad omen and viewed with trepidation in the Aztec civilization, Xolotl was the patron god of twins and individuals with physical abnormalities, which were a subject of fascination in Mesoamerican cultures.",
      "locator": "Patron of twins",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Meskipun kembar umumnya dianggap pertanda buruk dan dipandang dengan waswas dalam peradaban Aztec, Xolotl adalah dewa pelindung kembar dan orang dengan kelainan fisik, yang menjadi bahan ketertarikan dalam budaya Mesoamerika.",
        "en": "Although twins were generally seen as a bad omen in Aztec civilization, Xolotl was the patron god of twins and individuals with physical abnormalities, a subject of fascination in Mesoamerican cultures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c11",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s2",
      "quote": "Xolotl accompanied Quetzalcoatl on his mission to retrieve bones from the underworld in order to create humanity",
      "locator": "Xolotl and Quetzalcoatl",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xolotl menemani Quetzalcoatl dalam misinya mengambil tulang-tulang dari dunia bawah untuk menciptakan umat manusia.",
        "en": "Xolotl accompanied Quetzalcoatl on his mission to retrieve bones from the underworld in order to create humanity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c12",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s2",
      "quote": "While most of the gods willingly offer themselves for sacrifice, setting a mythic precedent for the Aztec culture of human sacrifice, Xolotl attempts to escape.",
      "locator": "Fifth sun myth",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika sebagian besar dewa rela mengorbankan diri, Xolotl berusaha melarikan diri.",
        "en": "While most of the gods willingly offer themselves for sacrifice, Xolotl attempts to escape."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c13",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s2",
      "quote": "Xolotl makes his first attempt to escape by turning into a double maize plant. When he is discovered in that guise, he turns into a doubled maguey plant, and when that transformation fails to conceal him, he turns into a salamander still known today by its Nahuatl name, axolotl.",
      "locator": "Fifth sun myth",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xolotl pertama kali mencoba kabur dengan menjadi tanaman jagung ganda. Ketika ketahuan, ia menjadi tanaman maguey ganda, dan ketika itu pun gagal menyembunyikannya, ia menjadi salamander yang hingga kini dikenal dengan nama Nahuatl axolotl.",
        "en": "Xolotl first tries to escape by turning into a double maize plant; when discovered he becomes a doubled maguey plant, and when that fails to conceal him he becomes a salamander still known by its Nahuatl name, axolotl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c14",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s2",
      "quote": "Even in the form of an axolotl, Xolotl is ultimately caught and sacrificed along with the rest of the gods",
      "locator": "Fifth sun myth",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bahkan dalam wujud axolotl, Xolotl akhirnya tertangkap dan dikorbankan bersama dewa-dewa lainnya.",
        "en": "Even in the form of an axolotl, Xolotl is ultimately caught and sacrificed along with the rest of the gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c15",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s2",
      "quote": "dogs, perhaps embodying the role of Xolotl, guided their masters into the Underworld after death and were of particular use in crossing bodies of water",
      "locator": "Dogs and the afterlife",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anjing, yang mungkin menjelmakan peran Xolotl, menuntun tuannya ke Dunia Bawah setelah mati dan sangat berguna untuk menyeberangi perairan.",
        "en": "Dogs, perhaps embodying the role of Xolotl, guided their masters into the Underworld after death and were of particular use in crossing bodies of water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c16",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s2",
      "quote": "The word axolotl is a combination of the Nahuatl terms xolotl (explained above) and atl, meaning \"water,\" and so could be roughly translated as \"water twin.\"",
      "locator": "Fifth sun myth",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata axolotl adalah gabungan istilah Nahuatl xolotl dan atl yang berarti \"air\", sehingga kira-kira dapat diterjemahkan sebagai \"kembar air\".",
        "en": "The word axolotl is a combination of the Nahuatl terms xolotl and atl, meaning \"water\", and so could be roughly translated as \"water twin\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c17",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s2",
      "quote": "An homage to Xolotl also appears in the 2017 Disney film Coco in the form of a dog poignantly named Dante.",
      "locator": "Xolotl in Modern Culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penghormatan kepada Xolotl juga muncul dalam film Disney Coco (2017) berupa seekor anjing bernama Dante.",
        "en": "An homage to Xolotl also appears in the 2017 Disney film Coco in the form of a dog named Dante."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "xolotl-c18",
      "source_id": "xolotl-s1",
      "quote": "Xolotl was the patron of the Mesoamerican ballgame. Some scholars argue the ballgame symbolizes the Sun's perilous and uncertain nighttime journey through the underworld. Xolotl is able to help in the Sun's rebirth since he possesses the power to enter and exit the underworld.",
      "locator": "Ollin and Xolotl",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xolotl adalah pelindung permainan bola Mesoamerika. Sebagian sarjana berpendapat permainan itu melambangkan perjalanan malam Matahari yang berbahaya melalui dunia bawah; Xolotl dapat membantu kelahiran kembali Matahari karena ia berkuasa keluar-masuk dunia bawah.",
        "en": "Xolotl was the patron of the Mesoamerican ballgame, which some scholars say symbolizes the Sun's perilous nighttime journey through the underworld; Xolotl can help the Sun's rebirth because he has the power to enter and exit the underworld."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Chalchiuhtlicue adalah dewi Aztec untuk danau, aliran air, kesuburan, dan persalinan, yang juga dianggap melindungi perempuan dan anak-anak.",
    "en": "Chalchiuhtlicue is the Aztec goddess of lakes and streams, fertility, and childbirth, thought to protect women and children.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Chalchiuhtlicue adalah dewi danau dan aliran air dalam agama Aztec. Ia dikaitkan dengan kesuburan, menjadi pelindung persalinan, dan termasuk kelompok besar dewa hujan Aztec yang berkerabat dengan dewa air Chalchiuhtlatonal. Bersama Tlaloc, ia disebut membantu memerintah kerajaan firdaus Tlalocan.",
      "en": "Chalchiuhtlicue is the Aztec goddess of lakes and streams. She is linked with fertility, acts as patroness of childbirth, and belongs to a larger group of Aztec rain gods closely related to the water god Chalchiuhtlatonal. Together with Tlaloc she is said to help rule the paradisial kingdom of Tlalocan.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chalchiuhtlicue-c01",
        "chalchiuhtlicue-c02",
        "chalchiuhtlicue-c03",
        "chalchiuhtlicue-c11"

    },
    {
      "id": "Ia membawa kesuburan bagi tanaman dan diyakini melindungi perempuan serta anak-anak; ia juga disebut penjaga bayi baru lahir. Selain itu ia memimpin ritus kelahiran, pemandian korban persembahan, penyucian peradilan, dan penobatan raja. Dalam sebuah gambar, cangkang kerang di tepi air menunjukkan kaitannya dengan kesuburan. Sumber-sumber berbeda menyebutnya istri atau saudari Tlaloc, dan dalam teks lain istri Xiuhtecuhtli.",
      "en": "Crops gain fertility through her, and women and children are believed to be under her protection; she is also called the guardian of newborns. In addition she presided over birth rituals, the bathing of sacrificial victims, judicial purification, and royal investiture. In one picture, seashells at the water's edge point to her link with fertility. Sources differ on whether she was wife or sister of Tlaloc, and other texts make her the wife of Xiuhtecuhtli.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chalchiuhtlicue-c04",
        "chalchiuhtlicue-c05",

        "chalchiuhtlicue-c12"

    {
      "id": "Menurut mitos ia pernah memakan matahari dan bulan, dan ia sering dikaitkan dengan ular. Kalender Aztec memuat lima perayaan besar bagi dirinya dan Tlaloc, ketika para imam menyelam ke danau dan menirukan katak. Dalam seni ia sering dipahat dari batu hijau sesuai namanya, yang paling lazim ditafsirkan sebagai \"dia yang berok batu giok\".",
      "en": "Myths say she once ate the sun and the moon, and she is often associated with serpents. In the Five Suns myth she presided over the Fourth Sun and is believed to have destroyed it with 52 years of rain in retaliation for Tlaloc's mistreatment. The Aztec calendar held five big celebrations for her and Tlaloc, during which priests dove into a lake and imitated frogs. In sculpture she is often carved from green stone, in keeping with her name, which is most commonly read as \"she of the jade skirt\".",

      "statement": {

      "source_id": "chalchiuhtlicue-s1",
      "quote": "Chalchiuitlicue directly translates to 'Jade her skirt'; however, her name is most commonly interpreted as 'she of the jade skirt'. She was also known as Chalchiuhtlatonac ... 'She who shines like jade' and Matlalcueye 'Possessor of the Blue Skirt' by the Tlaxcalans",

      "locator": "Religious significance",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

    {

      "source_id": "chalchiuhtlicue-s1",

      "locator": "Religious significance",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selain kematian yang berkaitan dengan air, Chalchiuhtlicue memimpin ritus kelahiran, pemandian korban persembahan dan pelaku upacara, penyucian peradilan, penobatan raja, dan pendaurulangan limbah ritual.",
        "en": "In addition to water-related deaths, Chalchiuhtlicue presided over birth rituals, the bathing of sacrificial victims and ceremonial actors, judicial purification, royal investiture, and the recycling of ritual waste."

    {
      "id": "chalchiuhtlicue-c09",
      "source_id": "chalchiuhtlicue-s1",
      "quote": "Chalchiutlicue was the guardian of the children and newborns. When children fell ill, healers called on the goddess as they practiced hydromancy in order to find the tonalli (spirits) of sick children.",
      "locator": "Childbirth",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chalchiutlicue (ejaan lain) adalah penjaga anak-anak dan bayi baru lahir. Ketika anak jatuh sakit, para tabib memanggil sang dewi sambil melakukan hidromansi untuk menemukan tonalli (roh) anak yang sakit.",
        "en": "Chalchiutlicue (a variant spelling) was the guardian of children and newborns; when children fell ill, healers called on the goddess while practicing hydromancy to find the tonalli (spirits) of the sick children."

      "locator": "Archaeological records",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "en": "When represented in sculpture, Chalchiutlicue (a variant spelling) is often carved from green stone in accordance with her name."

      "quote": "Chalchiuhtlicue was the Aztec goddess of lakes and streams. She was related to the liquid environment of the womb and played an important role in ceremonies related to birth.",
      "locator": "Image caption",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chalchiuhtlicue adalah dewi danau dan aliran air bangsa Aztec. Ia dikaitkan dengan lingkungan cair rahim dan berperan penting dalam upacara kelahiran.",
        "en": "Chalchiuhtlicue was the Aztec goddess of lakes and streams, related to the liquid environment of the womb and important in ceremonies related to birth."

      "locator": "Image caption",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",

      "locator": "Chalchihuitlicue (section heading)",

        "en": "In the source's section on Chalchihuitlicue (its spelling), the goddess was the wife of Tlaloc, god of rain and moisture; the name means Lady of the Emerald Robe, alluding to the colour of water, and she was specially worshipped by water-carriers and all whose work brought them into contact with water."

    {
      "id": "chalchiuhtlicue-c14",
      "source_id": "chalchiuhtlicue-s3",

      "locator": "Tlaloc (section)",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para imam Tlaloc menyelam ke danau sambil menirukan suara dan gerak katak, yang sebagai penghuni air berada di bawah perlindungan khusus sang dewa. Chalchihuitlicue (ejaan sumber), istrinya, sering dilambangkan dengan gambar kecil seekor katak.",

      "statement": {

      "source_id": "chalchiuhtlicue-s1",
      "quote": "Five of the 20 big celebrations in the Aztec calendar were dedicated to Chalchiutlicue and her husband (or brother) Tlaloc. During these celebrations, priests dove into a lake and imitated the movements and the croaking of frogs, hoping to bring rain.",

    "id": "Paul Bunyan adalah penebang kayu raksasa dan pahlawan rakyat dalam cerita rakyat Amerika dan Kanada, yang ditemani Babe si Sapi Biru.",
    "en": "Paul Bunyan is a giant lumberjack and folk hero of American and Canadian folklore, accompanied by Babe the Blue Ox.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Paul Bunyan adalah penebang kayu raksasa dan pahlawan rakyat dalam cerita rakyat Amerika dan Kanada. Cerita bualannya berkisar pada kerja-kerja supermanusiawinya, dan ia biasanya ditemani Babe si Sapi Biru. Dalam artikel-artikel awal ia dipuji sebagai penebang bertenaga fisik besar dan berketerampilan tiada banding.",
      "en": "In American and Canadian folklore Paul Bunyan appears as an enormous lumberjack and folk hero. His tall tales center on feats of superhuman labor, and he is customarily joined by Babe the Blue Ox. In early articles he is praised as a logger of great physical strength and unmatched skill.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "paul-bunyan-c01",
        "paul-bunyan-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tokoh ini berasal dari tradisi lisan para penebang kayu Amerika Utara; cerita-ceritanya di barak kamp penebangan berupa potongan pendek yang sebagian memuat motif dongeng lama. Satu sumber menyebut rujukan cetak pertama muncul pada 1893, sumber lain pada 1906. Beberapa peneliti berusaha menelusurinya ke tokoh Bon Jean dari cerita rakyat Kanada Prancis.",
      "en": "The character comes from the oral tradition of North American loggers; his stories in lumber-camp bunkhouses were short fragments, some with motifs from older folktales. One source puts his first print reference in 1893, another in 1906. Several researchers have tried to trace him to Bon Jean of French Canadian folklore.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "paul-bunyan-c02",

    {
      "id": "Penulis iklan William B. Laughead mempopulerkannya untuk Red River Lumber Company: ia menamai sapi biru \"Babe\", membuat gambar pertama Bunyan, dan membuatnya menjulang melampaui pepohonan, padahal dalam dongeng awal ia sangat tinggi dan kuat tetapi berukuran manusia. Cerita Laughead dan cerita lain mengaitkannya dengan terciptanya 10.000 danau Minnesota, Grand Canyon, dan Mount Hood. Laughead juga mengembangkan tokoh baru, termasuk Babe dan pengasuhnya Brimstone Bill. Orang Ojibwe mengadaptasi sosoknya menjadi cerita tentang Nanabozho.",
      "en": "The advertising writer William B. Laughead popularized him for the Red River Lumber Company: he named the blue ox \"Babe\", made the first picture of Bunyan, and made him tower over trees, whereas in early folktales he was very tall and strong but of human size. Laughead's writing and other stories credit him with the 10,000 lakes of Minnesota, the Grand Canyon, and Mount Hood. Laughead also developed new characters, including Babe and Babe's custodian Brimstone Bill. The Ojibwe adapted his figure into stories about Nanabozho.",

    "id": "Seiring cerita bualan itu meluas dari pantai ke pantai, Paul Bunyan tak lagi terbatas di hutan dan menjadi legenda semua kawasan. Perusahaan pemasarnya memakai namanya dalam bisnis dan mendaftarkan hak cipta ilustrasi kepalanya sebagai merek dagang.",
    "en": "As the tall tales spread coast to coast, Paul Bunyan was no longer restricted to the woods and became a legend of all regions. The company that marketed him used his name in business dealings and copyrighted an illustration of his head as a trademark.",

        "en": "Bunyan and Babe are supposed to have formed Minnesota's 10,000 lakes by stepping there; Bunyan is also credited with the Grand Canyon by dragging his ax and Mount Hood by piling stones on his campfire."
      },
      "claim_ids": [
        "paul-bunyan-c07",
        "paul-bunyan-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "Bagaimana kampanye iklan mengubah tokoh bualan para penebang kayu menjadi legenda nasional?",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c01",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "Paul Bunyan is a giant lumberjack and folk hero in American and Canadian folklore. His tall tales revolve around his superhuman labors, and he is customarily accompanied by Babe the Blue Ox (French: Babe le bœuf bleu ), his pet and working animal.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Paul Bunyan adalah penebang kayu raksasa dan pahlawan rakyat dalam cerita rakyat Amerika dan Kanada. Cerita-cerita bualannya berkisar pada kerja-kerja supermanusiawinya, dan ia biasanya ditemani Babe si Sapi Biru, hewan peliharaan sekaligus hewan kerjanya.",
        "en": "Paul Bunyan is a giant lumberjack and folk hero in American and Canadian folklore; his tall tales revolve around his superhuman labors, and he is customarily accompanied by Babe the Blue Ox, his pet and working animal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c02",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "Paul Bunyan is a giant lumberjack and folk hero in American and Canadian folklore. ... The character first appeared in print in 1893 but originated earlier in the oral tradition of North American loggers.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tokoh ini pertama kali muncul di media cetak pada 1893 menurut sumber ini, tetapi berasal lebih awal dari tradisi lisan para penebang kayu Amerika Utara.",
        "en": "According to this source the character first appeared in print in 1893 but originated earlier in the oral tradition of North American loggers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c03",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "Several researchers have attempted to trace Paul Bunyan to the character of Bon Jean of French Canadian folklore.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beberapa peneliti berusaha menelusuri Paul Bunyan ke tokoh Bon Jean dalam cerita rakyat Kanada Prancis.",
        "en": "Several researchers have attempted to trace Paul Bunyan to the character of Bon Jean of French Canadian folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c04",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "In contrast to the lengthy narratives abundant in published material, Paul Bunyan \"stories\" when told in the lumbercamp bunkhouses were presented in short fragments. Some of these stories include motifs from older folktales, such as absurdly severe weather and fearsome critters.",
      "locator": "Early references",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbeda dari narasi panjang dalam bahan terbitan, \"cerita\" Paul Bunyan yang dituturkan di barak kamp penebangan disajikan dalam potongan pendek. Sebagian cerita itu memuat motif dari dongeng lama, seperti cuaca yang sangat buruk dan makhluk menakutkan (fearsome critters).",
        "en": "In contrast to the lengthy narratives abundant in published material, Paul Bunyan \"stories\" told in lumbercamp bunkhouses were short fragments, some including motifs from older folktales such as absurdly severe weather and fearsome critters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c05",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "Paul Bunyan was more widely popularized by freelance writer William B. Laughead (1882–1958) in a 1916 promotional pamphlet for the Red River Lumber Company.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Paul Bunyan lebih luas dipopulerkan oleh penulis lepas William B. Laughead (1882–1958) lewat pamflet promosi tahun 1916 untuk Red River Lumber Company.",
        "en": "Paul Bunyan was more widely popularized by freelance writer William B. Laughead (1882–1958) in a 1916 promotional pamphlet for the Red River Lumber Company."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c06",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "Among other things, Laughead gave the name \"Babe\" to the blue ox, created the first pictorial representation of Bunyan, and increased Paul Bunyan's height to impossible proportions (in Laughead's version, Paul Bunyan towers over trees, while in the earlier folktales Bunyan had been extraordinarily tall and strong, but of human proportions).",
      "locator": "Laughead's influence",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Antara lain, Laughead memberi nama \"Babe\" pada sapi biru, membuat gambar pertama Bunyan, dan menambah tinggi Paul Bunyan hingga mustahil (dalam versi Laughead ia menjulang melampaui pepohonan, sedangkan dalam dongeng lebih awal Bunyan sangat tinggi dan kuat tetapi berukuran manusia).",
        "en": "Among other things Laughead named the blue ox \"Babe\", created the first pictorial representation of Bunyan, and raised Paul Bunyan's height to impossible proportions (towering over trees in his version, whereas in earlier folktales Bunyan was extraordinarily tall and strong but of human proportions)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c07",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "Laughead attributes the creation of several American landscapes, landmarks and natural wonders to Paul Bunyan. He wrote that Paul Bunyan and Babe are said to have created the 10,000 lakes of Minnesota by their footprints.",
      "locator": "Laughead's influence",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Laughead mengaitkan terciptanya beberapa lanskap, tengara, dan keajaiban alam Amerika dengan Paul Bunyan. Ia menulis bahwa Paul Bunyan dan Babe dikatakan menciptakan 10.000 danau Minnesota lewat jejak kaki mereka.",
        "en": "Laughead attributes the creation of several American landscapes, landmarks, and natural wonders to Paul Bunyan, writing that Paul Bunyan and Babe are said to have created the 10,000 lakes of Minnesota with their footprints."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c08",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "Stories about Bunyan credited him with creating the Grand Canyon by pulling his ax behind him, and Mount Hood by putting stones on his campfire.",
      "locator": "Laughead's influence",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita tentang Bunyan menyebut ia menciptakan Grand Canyon dengan menyeret kapaknya di belakangnya, dan Mount Hood dengan menumpuk batu di atas api unggunnya.",
        "en": "Stories about Bunyan credited him with creating the Grand Canyon by pulling his ax behind him and Mount Hood by putting stones on his campfire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c09",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "The first known reference of Paul Bunyan in print appeared in the March 17, 1893, issue of Gladwin County Record.",
      "locator": "Early references",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rujukan tercetak pertama yang diketahui tentang Paul Bunyan muncul dalam terbitan 17 Maret 1893 Gladwin County Record.",
        "en": "The first known reference to Paul Bunyan in print appeared in the March 17, 1893, issue of the Gladwin County Record."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c10",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "In all the articles, Paul Bunyan is praised as a logger of great physical strength and unrivaled skill.",
      "locator": "Early references",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam semua artikel itu, Paul Bunyan dipuji sebagai penebang kayu bertenaga fisik besar dan berketerampilan tiada banding.",
        "en": "In all the articles Paul Bunyan is praised as a logger of great physical strength and unrivaled skill."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c11",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "The figure of Bunyan was adapted by the Ojibwe people into folklore about Nanabozho, a culture hero of the Ojibwe and Anishinaabe.",
      "locator": "In Ojibwe folklore",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sosok Bunyan diadaptasi orang Ojibwe menjadi cerita rakyat tentang Nanabozho, pahlawan budaya orang Ojibwe dan Anishinaabe.",
        "en": "The figure of Bunyan was adapted by the Ojibwe people into folklore about Nanabozho, a culture hero of the Ojibwe and Anishinaabe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c12",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "There are many hypotheses about the etymology of the name Paul Bunyan. Much of the commentary focuses on a French-Canadian origin for the name.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada banyak hipotesis tentang asal-usul nama Paul Bunyan. Sebagian besar ulasan berfokus pada asal Prancis-Kanada bagi nama itu.",
        "en": "There are many hypotheses about the etymology of the name Paul Bunyan, and much of the commentary focuses on a French-Canadian origin for the name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c13",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s1",
      "quote": "His pet joke and the one with which the green horn at the camp is sure to be tried, consists of a series of imaginative tales about the year Paul Bunyan lumbered in North Dakota. The great Paul is represented as getting out countless millions of timber in the year of the \"blue snow\".",
      "locator": "Early references",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lelucon andalannya, yang pasti dipakai untuk menguji orang baru di kamp, berupa serangkaian cerita khayal tentang tahun ketika Paul Bunyan menebang kayu di North Dakota. Sang Paul digambarkan menghasilkan jutaan kayu pada tahun \"salju biru\".",
        "en": "The camp joke is a series of imaginative tales about the year Paul Bunyan lumbered in North Dakota, in which the great Paul gets out countless millions of timber in the year of the \"blue snow\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c14",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s2",
      "quote": "Paul Bunyan first appeared in print in 1906 but languished in relative obscurity until Laughead's efforts for the Red River advertising campaign.",
      "locator": "Tall Tales and Lumber Sales",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Paul Bunyan pertama kali muncul di media cetak pada 1906 menurut sumber ini, tetapi tetap relatif tak dikenal sampai upaya Laughead untuk kampanye iklan Red River.",
        "en": "According to this source Paul Bunyan first appeared in print in 1906 but languished in relative obscurity until Laughead's efforts for the Red River advertising campaign."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c15",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s2",
      "quote": "Finally, Walker suggested the folk hero Paul Bunyan, stories of whom circulated forestry camps, especially in the Great Lakes region.",
      "locator": "Tall Tales and Lumber Sales",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akhirnya Walker mengusulkan pahlawan rakyat Paul Bunyan, yang ceritanya beredar di kamp-kamp kehutanan, terutama di kawasan Great Lakes.",
        "en": "Finally Walker suggested the folk hero Paul Bunyan, stories of whom circulated in forestry camps, especially in the Great Lakes region."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c16",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s2",
      "quote": "Through his text and illustrations, Laughead made significant contributions to the Bunyan canon ... developing wholly new characters, including Babe the Blue Ox and Babe's custodian Brimstone Bill; and bringing minor characters into distincition, including the penny-pinching clerk Johnny Inkslinger and camp cooks Big Joe.",
      "locator": "Tall Tales and Lumber Sales",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lewat teks dan ilustrasinya, Laughead menyumbang banyak pada kanon Bunyan, termasuk menciptakan tokoh baru seperti Babe si Sapi Biru dan pengasuhnya Brimstone Bill, serta menonjolkan tokoh kecil seperti juru tulis pelit Johnny Inkslinger dan juru masak kamp Big Joe.",
        "en": "Through his text and illustrations Laughead made significant contributions to the Bunyan canon, developing new characters including Babe the Blue Ox and Babe's custodian Brimstone Bill, and bringing minor characters into focus such as the penny-pinching clerk Johnny Inkslinger and camp cook Big Joe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c17",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s2",
      "quote": "As the tall tales stretched coast to coast, Paul the Lumberjack-Hero was dwarfed by Paul the American Spirit. No longer restricted to the woods, Paul Bunyan became a legend of all regions. Stories credited the lumberman with varied accomplishments, from digging the Puget Sound to succeeding as a Southwest oilman to building the Panama Canal.",
      "locator": "Tall Tales and Lumber Sales",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika cerita bualan itu meluas dari pantai ke pantai, Paul Sang Pahlawan Penebang Kayu terkalahkan oleh Paul Sang Semangat Amerika. Tak lagi terbatas di hutan, Paul Bunyan menjadi legenda semua kawasan. Cerita menyebut penebang itu menggali Puget Sound, sukses sebagai pengusaha minyak di Barat Daya, hingga membangun Terusan Panama.",
        "en": "As the tall tales spread coast to coast, Paul the Lumberjack-Hero was dwarfed by Paul the American Spirit; no longer restricted to the woods, Paul Bunyan became a legend of all regions, credited with deeds from digging Puget Sound to succeeding as a Southwest oilman to building the Panama Canal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c18",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s2",
      "quote": "The company officially adopted Paul Bunyan's name for use in its business dealings and copyrighted Laughead's illustration of Paul Bunyan's head, using it as a trademark for its products.",
      "locator": "Tall Tales and Lumber Sales",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perusahaan itu secara resmi memakai nama Paul Bunyan dalam urusan bisnisnya dan mendaftarkan hak cipta ilustrasi kepala Paul Bunyan karya Laughead, memakainya sebagai merek dagang produknya.",
        "en": "The company officially adopted Paul Bunyan's name for use in its business dealings and copyrighted Laughead's illustration of Paul Bunyan's head, using it as a trademark for its products."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c19",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s2",
      "quote": "A postcard-sized booklet Introducing Mr. Paul Bunyan of Westwood, California was published in 1914, and its companion Tales About Paul Bunyan, Volume II was printed just two years later.",
      "locator": "Tall Tales and Lumber Sales",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Buklet seukuran kartu pos Introducing Mr. Paul Bunyan of Westwood, California terbit pada 1914, dan pasangannya Tales About Paul Bunyan, Volume II dicetak dua tahun kemudian.",
        "en": "A postcard-sized booklet, Introducing Mr. Paul Bunyan of Westwood, California, was published in 1914, and its companion, Tales About Paul Bunyan, Volume II, was printed two years later."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paul-bunyan-c20",
      "source_id": "paul-bunyan-s2",
      "quote": "In 1922, the company published The Marvelous Exploits of Paul Bunyan, a re-vamped version of Laughead's first work.",
      "locator": "Tall Tales and Lumber Sales",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1922 perusahaan itu menerbitkan The Marvelous Exploits of Paul Bunyan, versi yang dirombak dari karya pertama Laughead.",
        "en": "In 1922 the company published The Marvelous Exploits of Paul Bunyan, a re-vamped version of Laughead's first work."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Ogopogo adalah monster danau yang dikatakan mendiami Danau Okanagan di British Columbia, Kanada, dan berakar pada kepercayaan First Nations tentang roh danau.",
    "en": "In British Columbia, Canada, Ogopogo is the monster believed to live in Okanagan Lake, and its roots lie in First Nations belief in a lake spirit.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ogopogo dikatakan mendiami Danau Okanagan di British Columbia, Kanada. Kisah tentang makhluk besar di danau itu berusia ratusan tahun dan dituturkan orang First Nations setempat, yang menganggapnya roh danau yang suci; orang Syilx menyebutnya N'ha-a-itk dan orang Secwepemc dan Syilx juga menyebutnya Naitaka. Sebagian sarjana menelusuri perkembangannya dari cerita rakyat First Nations dan motif umum monster air.",
      "en": "Ogopogo is said to live in Okanagan Lake in British Columbia, Canada. Stories of a large creature in the lake are hundreds of years old and were told by local First Nations people, who considered it a sacred lake spirit; the Syilx call it N'ha-a-itk, and the Secwepemc and Syilx also called it Naitaka. Some scholars trace its development from First Nations folklore and widespread water-monster motifs.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ogopogo-c01",
        "ogopogo-c07",
        "ogopogo-c15",
        "ogopogo-c17"

    {
      "id": "Kebanyakan kesaksian menggambarkannya sebagai makhluk menyerupai ular, hingga sekitar 15 meter, berkulit gelap licin, dengan tubuh lebih tebal daripada tiang telepon dan kepala mirip kuda atau kambing. Ia dikatakan bergerak sangat cepat dengan ekornya yang kuat, dan dalam tradisi pribumi memakai ekornya menimbulkan badai untuk menenggelamkan korban. Sumber berbeda pendapat apakah ia entitas jahat yang menuntut persembahan atau roh suci yang melindungi lembah.",
      "en": "Most accounts describe it as a snake-like creature up to about 15 meters long with smooth dark skin, a body thicker than a telephone pole, and a head like a horse or goat. It is said to move extremely fast with a powerful tail, and in native lore used its tail to raise storms and drown victims. Sources disagree on whether it is an evil entity demanding sacrifice or a sacred spirit that protects the valley.",

      "id": "Penampakan pertama oleh pemukim tercatat pada 1855, dan penulis Susan Allison melaporkan melihat sesuatu pada 1872. Nama Ogopogo diambil dari lagu music-hall Inggris pada 1920-an. Sejak itu Ogopogo menjadi atraksi wisata dan memicu hadiah satu juta dolar; Greenpeace bahkan menyatakan ia harus difilmkan, bukan ditangkap; ekspedisi ilmiah 1991 tidak menemukan tanda-tandanya, dan penjelasan yang diajukan mencakup ikan besar seperti sturgeon, fenomena geologi danau, atau batang kayu.",
      "en": "The first settler sighting is recorded in 1855, and the author Susan Allison reported seeing something in 1872. The name Ogopogo was taken from an English music-hall song in the 1920s.       "source_id": "ogopogo-s1",
      "quote": "Okanagan Lake is the largest of five inter-connected freshwater fjord lakes in the Okanagan Valley in British Columbia.         "en": "Okanagan Lake is the largest of five inter-connected freshwater fjord lakes in the Okanagan Valley, named after the Syilx Okanagan Nation who have lived there since time immemorial, and created when melting glaciers flooded a valley 10,000 years ago."
